| 4.17 | **Configurations — done 2026-10-11 (Linux), at the user's request** (C98-C113, [configurations.md](configurations.md), W16 of [engine-facts.md](engine-facts.md), group A of the test plan, A01-A71; the GUI suite `configurations`, 48 cases, TC-CLI-022 to -033, and the Live tab cases recalibrated): the user: "I need to drive the QuickAI by configuration/s ... imported/exported/selected ... models, command line arguments, and other settings ... shared with others so the same runtime is available there also ... warn about incompatibility ... a new section (similar to settings) to manage multiple configurations ... a top-level configuration dropdown ... a default ... save by a different name ... We need data-driven design ... for something like Open WebUI the environment may not be mutable, so leave this out if not possible", and, to the plan: "Start impl". **Built:** the `profile` kind that Q10 reserved is a configuration: a data-tier document, one file in `profiles/` of the person's configuration directory, a built-in Default; what a configuration holds, leaves out or asks about is declared by the engine documents (`share` on `[[field]]`, by type when silent) and the sentences of what a person is told are `[[finding]]` of the vocabulary, so that no engine, field or program is named in the code (A54, A65 read it); models are references and builds are labels, and **a configuration selects and never installs**; `settings.json` stays the working state and a configuration is saved on purpose (Save, Save as, an amber • for unsaved changes, a question when a choice would lose them), with the first start keeping what the person had set as their own Default; a file from outside is read before it is taken (what asks is taken only when ticked, a line that would make a program load other code is refused whatever the file says, limits, no network); the dropdown of the title row and a window of its own (C87's way: list, details, notes with their buttons, Use, Save here, Rename, Duplicate, Export, Import, Delete, Reset to factory, Revert); `quickai configuration list|show|check|export|import`, `plan --configuration`, `config docs profile`. **Open WebUI** gives a configuration only what it reads at every start (whether it starts with Ollama, its port, its internet access) and never its environment: **W16 was run for real** (a second start beside another Ollama listed none of its models: it keeps the address of its first start), so QuickAI remembers that address (the field its document names) and says when a later start would give another. **Found by running it, all fixed:** the first start's Default pinned a model nobody chose and showed an unsaved mark at once (A69); the narrowest window with the live tabs on covered the last tab with the settings icon (A70); a model said as a file and as the entry of the recommended list were two models (A71); "1 lines that look like secrets" (C113); the code naming a companion's field (C111). **Revised the same day (WP4.17.9, C114-C116), after the user's screenshot of the first version:** "move the dropdown to a different section, doesn't look good at the location - right next to the header. Additionally, when user makes an edit, it has to show the configuration as edited - or find other way to specify that it has changed. i.e. If I load a configuration, it is a fact - but the moment I change, It should convey that it is modified" -> the dropdown is a section of the Main page under the title row (the title row is as it was before configurations); while the window holds what was not saved the section says **• Modified** in amber, in an amber outline, with **Save**, **Save as...** and **Revert** (which asks, in the main window), and nothing under it moves (A72-A74); and looking at the screenshot found that choosing a configuration whose build is not installed said Modified at once with a hover that listed nothing, so "modified" is now one answer made on the values in force (A75, C116). **Not done:** Windows and macOS (nothing was run there); `argument_unknown` and a comparison of versions (they wait for WP4.4); a fixed loopback address for Open WebUI that would never go stale. | A01-A75; GUI `configurations` (TC-CNF-001 to -058), TC-CLI-022 to -033 |
| 4.18 | **Headless: `quickai-run`, and the split that made it possible — done 2026-10-12 (Linux), at the user's request** (C117-C124, [headless.md](headless.md), L24 and L25 of [engine-facts.md](engine-facts.md), group O of the test plan, O01-O73): the user, who runs ComfyUI on a rented server: "The server is you - quickai - But I am on a headless device - the server is rented. Can I make use of the configuration here to run a headless version of quickAI there?", then, to my answer that there was no headless mode: "what about creating another cli application that can run with a given configuration - it has to get all the info from the config, download/configure itself and start?", and "Go for it. make things modular in the current QuickAI, test, then go for implementations." **Built, in that order:** (1) the **split**: `quickai-runtime` took every module that does anything to the computer (47,000 lines with their tests, fakes, embedded documents and assets, by `git mv`), the window kept `app/` and `main.rs`, and two guard tests hold the line (O01, O02); proved to change nothing by comparing the whole test list and the output of 28 headless commands before and after; (2) three pieces of logic that would have been copied became one each: `resolve::resolve` (the `plan` command's body), `models::catalog_fetch` (the choice inside *Get*) and `acquire::pick_build`; (3) **cards found without `lspci`** (O40-O43); (4) **`provision::up`**: read, review, plan, install, fetch, start, pull and load, start the program that goes with the engine, say where, wait, stop it first (O10-O25, against a fake GitHub, a file server and a stand-in `llama-server` that really listens, with the shipped documents patched as a person patches them); (5) **`quickai-run`**: `up`, `prepare`, `check`, text or JSON lines, a ready file, SIGTERM and Ctrl-C, codes 0/1/2/3/4/130, 10 MB, links only libc, libgcc_s and the old libdl/libpthread/librt stubs, glibc 2.18 floor (O30-O38, the real binary); packaged by `build/headless.sh` in the base repository. **Found by running it for real** (the checks of the plan on the live GitHub and PyPI feeds for llama.cpp, Ollama with Open WebUI, and ComfyUI with the LTX video model; and the real llama.cpp b11564 and the real `LFM2.5-350M-Q4_K_M.gguf`, installed, fetched, started, asked a question that it answered, and stopped with SIGTERM, exit 0; and the packaged binary in a bare Debian 12 container): the rule that chose an OpenVINO build for a machine with no card (C119, L24, O63); the missing system library of a minimal install is in the failure's sentence (`libssl.so.3`); a mutation of each of 20 pieces of the code (the stop flag, the order of stopping, the guard question, the room check, the ready file, the signal handler, ...) was seen to fail a test, and two tests that **hung** instead of failing when the code under them was broken were bounded. **Not done:** Windows (C124); a real NVIDIA card (the CUDA path is read from the documents and the fakes; the card here is AMD's: L24); a real ComfyUI install through the program (the plan of it is checked against the live feeds; the install is the window's primitive, run for real on 2026-10-08, and takes 20 minutes and 17 GB); the models of a ComfyUI configuration are not written by the window's Export (it writes one), so a configuration for ComfyUI gets its `[[model]]` entries by hand. | O01-O73 |
| 4.19 | **A configuration that runs on any computer: where the files come from, the program that goes with the engine, and what is the computer's — done 2026-10-12 (Linux), at the user's request** (C125-C133, [headless.md](headless.md) sections 6-9, L26 and L27 of [engine-facts.md](engine-facts.md), group Y of the test plan, Y01-Y69, and the Robot suites `headless_*` of the GUI repository, TC-RUN-001 to -111): the user, after 4.18 (their words are in headless.md): "The configuration should never carry a model, it should just carry the information about where to fetch it from and the runtime will autofetch - or the user should have the ability to specify the location or provide alternate means to get it. If a configuration uses additional runtimes e.g. the open chat for ollama - the configuration should specify that as well and must be able to download and configure that too - The environmet specific variables if any are to be specified by the user - either through a configuration file(which you ask the user to fill) or via commandline etc. apart from that make necssary e2e tests with robot - test/ - and make the tests." **Built, tests first in each layer:** (1) the **file**: a file model says where it can be had from (`from`, `path`, `revision`, `into`, `url`; none takes a path of a computer), a configuration says what it needs (`[[needs]]`) and which build of a program that goes with the engine (`[runtime.<program>]`), the generated reference page and the window's Save and Export follow (Save keeps the models beyond the one it shows, the needs and the pins; Export says where in the repository a file is and at which revision); (2) the **environment** (`profile/environment.rs`, pure): the file (`[fetch]`, `[values]`, `[env]`, `[models]`), `--set`, `--env`, `--model-source`, layers, what is told of it with no secret, what a configuration still needs, and the file to fill in made from the engine documents (a fifth engine that is only a document takes part and a source scan names none); (3) the **plan**: a file is fetched from the address its place makes, or linked from the person's own file or a folder they point at after it is checked, or fetched from another address, repository or a mirror, or (offline) said with where it goes; a login goes to the hub and no other host; the review asks only when a place a file named is about to be used; a program the person names is used with no install; (4) the **binary**: `init`, the new words, code 5 and code 2, `Environment` events and the files of a step in the JSON, `HF_TOKEN`, `HF_ENDPOINT`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`; (5) six **Robot suites** that run the real program in a world of fakes (a GitHub with the archives of llama.cpp and Ollama, a hub that remembers who asked and with what login and sends a file to another host, a package index, the fake Python, the fake engines), 69 cases and 24 tests of the fakes. **Found by writing and running:** a `Debug` of a line of environment printed its value (Y23); the first rule of the review let a file of the wrong size in a folder stand for the person's yes (Y33); the sentence about a folder was cut at 60 characters (the Robot suite); a crash 1 s after Open WebUI answers is a race between code 1 and 4 (the case kills it from outside); the real hub sends every large file to a signed address on another host (L26); ComfyUI lists a linked model (L27). **Mutations:** every piece of the new code that decides something was broken on purpose and a test was seen to fail (see the run log); three tests were added for what got past. **Run for real** (see the run log): the user's own three Wan 2.2 files checked against the hub's SHA-256 and linked into a private home by `quickai-run prepare`; `check` of the worked example against the live documents. **Not done:** a real gated repository (no login at hand), a real NVIDIA card, a real ComfyUI install through the program, Windows; several models in the window (it keeps them while the first stays the first); a hook that runs a command to fetch (C127). | Y01-Y69; TC-RUN-001..111 |
| 4.20 | **What the computer has to have for a configuration: looked at before anything is fetched, installed when the person says so, and the installed environment tried before it is used — done 2026-10-13 (Linux), at the user's request** (C134-C140, [headless.md](headless.md) section 12, F29-F31 of [engine-facts.md](engine-facts.md), group Y of the test plan, Y71-Y99d, and the Robot suite `headless_prerequisites` of the GUI repository, TC-RUN-112 to -129): the first job sent to the rented server (MiniMax H3, int8 weights) died after 3.4 s because Triton could not build its helper, `Python.h` was missing, and the message that reached the sender said only that gcc "returned non-zero exit status 1". The user: "QuickAI - should ensure that the environment is setup correctly [...] The H3 profile should declare its system prerequisites: a C compiler, the Python headers (here python3.12-dev) and an NVIDIA driver of the 580 series or newer. quickai-run up should check them, then install them or stop with the exact command. It should also run a small self-test before saying "ready", for example compiling Triton's helper." And, while the tests were written: "emulate installation failures for python too" (read as: the package of Python's headers that cannot be installed, one that installs the headers of another Python, Python's venv module (`python3-venv`, the first thing that stopped the server) missing, pip failing, a Python that cannot be run; the venv module became a prerequisite of its own). **Built:** the engine's document names `[[prerequisite]]` entries (an id, a label, a condition on the computer, a check, the packages by package manager, a hint) and a configuration asks for them in `[[needs]] system = "<id>"` (the core: document types and their checks at load, `NeedKind::System`, `prerequisite.rs`, the reference page); the runtime looks (`host.rs`: a program on the `PATH` or the one `CC` names, a module a Python imports, the header file of a Python, the driver's version, the package manager, who may install, the install itself, a script in an environment), `provision/prerequisites.rs` joins it to the run (the look before the plan, the install or the stop with the command, the test of the environment after the install and before the first fetch), `quickai-run` has `--install-system-packages`, `--skip-prerequisites`, the event kinds `prerequisites` and `self_test`, and the code 6; ComfyUI's document names five prerequisites and ships the script of the test (`assets/comfyui/selftest/triton_kernel.py`), and the six configurations of video models say which they need (the generator `tools/build_configs.py` of the package writes them so). *Run for real, as far as this computer allows:* the script ran with the user's own ComfyUI environment (Triton 3.8.0 on the AMD card) in 4.2 s, and with the same interpreter copied without its `include/` folder it reproduced the failure that started this work, gcc's `fatal error: Python.h: No such file or directory` and Triton's `returned non-zero exit status 1`, in 2.2 s; the real `quickai-run prepare` printed that under "failed a test", with the configuration's words and the document's hint; `check` of the five configurations gave exit 6 on a computer with no NVIDIA driver and left the home empty. *Not run on the development computer:* installing a package for real (it would have changed it; only stand-ins of the package manager and of `sudo`) and an NVIDIA card. *Run for real later the same day on the NVIDIA server it was made for* (the run log, [headless.md](headless.md) section 11): `check` named the one missing piece, `Python.h`, and the command; `prepare --install-system-packages` installed `python3.12-dev` itself; the test of the environment passed on the L40S before the first model file was fetched (2.0 s by hand); the whole install took 11 min 7 s. *Then:* apt's install is told to wait for a lock another process holds (Y78c, C138, F32). *Found by running:* the closing sentence offered an install when only a driver was missing (Y99b), and the failure's words had to be on one stream to come in order. *Not done:* the window shows none of it; only ComfyUI's document names prerequisites (llama.cpp's CUDA build wants the same driver); no Python is downloaded. |
| 4.21 | **The window: the same shape, regrouped and restyled — done 2026-10-23, at the user's request** (C141-C147, group I of the test plan, I01-I03 and I10-I15, and the GUI suites recalibrated, [the test repository's docs/01_launch_and_window.md](../../test/docs/01_launch_and_window.md)): the user said the window "looks a bit dull" and asked for several looks and uses to choose from, "change the widgets if needed, also their places in the gui" and "think out of the box too.. get wild"; six directions were drawn on a private canvas from real screenshots of the window (a polish of today's, a sidebar, a launcher of configurations, the icon's bonsai as the state, a patch bay and a console), and the answer was "Lets stick to classic, but lets turn things around a bit and make it more user friendly - try to fit appropriately the widgets at the right locations". **Built:** `app/theme.rs` (the two palettes, the style, the card, the alert, the chip, the pill and the roles of a button), the title row (the logo made from the application's icon, the state in a pill, the three icons with a frame when the pointer is on them), the card of the model (the picker with the speed chip and the box that gets another model under it, one card for what were two boxes both called **Model:**), the card of the run options (dim, with the reason), the buttons (the one that matters in green, the one that ends in red), the alerts, the log in a well that keeps its room (but for while the results of a look-up or the way to connect an app are open, when a download that failed says so in a card under the box), the status line under the buttons when it is too long for their row, the list of builds scrolled into view when a look-up ends, the Live tab and the windows in the same theme, the Settings window opening at 500x700 (its controls are taller; it was 500x620) and the About window's text scrolling in what is left of the window; the panels' functions, their texts (but for the label **Get:**, the missing arrow of two sentences and the About's and the Settings' mention of the box) and their order are as they were. **Not built:** a face of its own (the fonts egui ships are kept), the choice of engine next to the configuration, and a model's deletion at the picker: each is a decision of its own. | Q7's reason (the OCR crops) is met in the same change; **Gate.**
- [ ] fmt, clippy, tests green
- [ ] new suites pass with fake engines and recipes (no GPU, no model needed)
- [ ] a vision recipe plans `--mmproj` and `--no-mmproj-offload` correctly; a build lacking the flag hides the field
- [ ] a manual smoke test with a real vision model on llama.cpp
- [ ] llama.cpp-only users see no change unless a recipe or document asks

## 10. Phase 5: layout, policy, strings, tooling

**Goal.** What remains hard-coded becomes data, and the tooling that keeps documents honest is complete.

**Entry.** Phase 4 gate. **Size.** M.

| WP | Work |
|---|---|
| 5.1 | `policy` document (paths, network allow-list, limits, timeouts, heuristics, GPU patterns) and one `net` layer that enforces it for every fetching primitive; the Appendix table of `architecture.md` is the checklist |
| 5.2 | `ui` document: window, screens, panels, theme, strings; the default layout *is* today's layout (§9.4); the shell (window, tab strip, log pane) becomes data last |
| 5.3 | Hot reload when idle and a "Reload configuration" action; configuration diagnostics panel |
| 5.4 | `bundle.toml` written by `stage_bundle` in `quickai_base` from `config.env` (what ships, with hashes); the `packaged_bundle` suite checks it |
| 5.5 | Generated reference documentation and JSON Schema from the Rust types; the generated privacy table checked against `PRIVACY.md` |
| 5.6 | Ranged download of the wanted entries of the Windows Ollama zip (about 0.11 GB instead of 1.47 GB) |
| 5.7 | `--features locked-config` (Q15) |

**Gate.** `PRIVACY.md` checked against the generated table; the pixel comparison identical for the default `ui`
document; the GUI suite at the baseline; `config check --strict` and the schema in CI.

## 11. Phase 6: optional

Several units and gateway routing for sidecars (whisper.cpp, stable-diffusion.cpp), remote content packs with
signing, the router-mode launch variant (§10.4; parked 2026-10-28 at the user's word, for a run on another computer is
QuickAI's own instead, C186), external drivers. Each is its own small plan, written when a
real need appears; none starts without a decision from the user.

### 12.2 Decision log

| Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-10-06 | Q1-Q15 accepted as recommended | the user |
| 2026-10-06 | C1-C10 chosen (§3.1) | open points of the architecture settled for the work packages |
| 2026-10-06 | The embedded `llamacpp.toml` holds only interpreted sections; hover texts and labels move in Phase 2 | a document should not promise behaviour that is not wired, and the code's copies of the texts are removed in Phase 2 anyway |
| 2026-10-06 | The `Status` job event waits for the Ollama pull (Phase 3) | no producer until then; an unused variant would be dead code |
| 2026-10-06 | The loader keeps contributor bodies and the registry falls back layer by layer | R6 and §5.3: an invalid user patch must leave the embedded document in force |
| 2026-10-06 | The proxy's internal port is a host fact allocated by the application before planning | keeps the planner pure; the failure message and behaviour are unchanged |
| 2026-10-06 | Expression trees are limited to 200 levels | found by the pathological-input test (stack overflow on a 5000-term chain) |
| 2026-10-06 | One full GUI suite run covers the Phase 0 and Phase 1 gates, taken on the Phase 1 build | Phase 0 is a strict subset of what the Phase 1 build contains; the per-phase pixel comparisons were run separately |
| 2026-10-06 | LoRA is a llama.cpp-only adapter kind: the Ollama engine document declares no `lora` component, so the Adapters panel never offers it there. A projector does work on Ollama (the spike: `files` carries it and `capabilities` gains `vision`, A14); a draft model is possible through `draft_files` (source only, O7) | Ollama v0.35.1 refuses adapters in `/api/create` (O7, read from `server/create.go`); architecture §10.2 had said `files` carries them and is corrected |
| 2026-10-06 | The Ollama context size travels only as `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH` in the spawn environment; the warm-up call no longer passes `num_ctx`, and the field is `spawn_only` | Spike A7: a per-request `num_ctx` reloads the runner at that size and the next request without it reloads it at the default, so on an attached daemon a warm-up value only moves the reload |
| 2026-10-06 | `OLLAMA_NO_CLOUD=1` is `locked` and protected from the free-text environment field | Spike A6: without it an idle `serve` contacts `ollama.com` within 40 ms and every ~4 h; with it a whole session made no non-loopback connection. Opting in to Ollama's cloud features would be a separate, explicit setting if ever wanted |
| 2026-10-06 | `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` uses SIGTERM, and Stop signals the whole process group | Spike A3: SIGKILL of `ollama serve` alone orphaned its `llama-server` runner (alive after 36 s); SIGTERM or a group kill removed both in 0.1 s |
| 2026-10-06 | The Ollama install skips the CUDA directories unless an NVIDIA GPU is known (`gpu.known`) | Spike A2: 2.2 GB installed against 102 MB without `cuda_v12` and `cuda_v13`; the download is 1.44 GB either way |
| 2026-10-06 | The widget registry of Phase 2 holds only the widgets a form uses today (integer and float drag boxes, port, checkbox, combo, one-line text, the runtime manager); key-value lines, the path picker, a progress bar and a button wait for Phase 3 | Nothing in the llama.cpp form needs them, and a widget with no user is code nobody has seen run (the plan listed them in WP2.3) |
| 2026-10-06 | `reset_on` and `ui.advanced` are not built; `validate` rules are written `{ check, message }` and the first whose check does not hold is the error | `recompute_on` and the `advanced` section cover what the first two were for; "check" says what must hold, where "when" would not say whether it is the error or the guard |
| 2026-10-06 | The speed estimate and the model recommendation take the engine's `gpu_offload` fact, not the GPU-layers number | That is what `derive` is for: Ollama's `gpu` choice derives the same fact, and neither heuristic should know which engine's setting it came from |
| 2026-10-06 | A platform the `[acquire]` words do not cover matches no build (the old code treated an unknown OS as Linux and an unknown architecture as x86-64) | Offering a Linux build to a BSD or a 32-bit ARM machine was a guess presented as a list; supported platforms are unchanged |
| 2026-10-06 | The old installer tests, which downloaded llama.cpp's source archives and wrote into the real `~/.quickai/llama-bin`, are replaced by tests against a local server of generated archives with a temporary root | The plan's Phase 0 note flagged them; a primitive that takes its root as a parameter can be tested without touching the user's home or the internet. The one real-network test that stays is the feed against GitHub (it checks the project's file naming) |
| 2026-10-06 | The first snapshot baseline (`base1`) is retired; the reference set for the 15 states is the one taken after the stall port and the crash-log order were pinned | `base1` predates the pinning and differs from every later run in two states for reasons of the harness, not the application |

| `quickai_base` (packaging) | 4 | macOS (WP4.25, C157, C158): `build/macos.sh` makes one universal app (the GUI for both Macs joined with `lipo`; llama.cpp's two macOS builds of the pinned release joined file by file into one `llama-server`, the 10 libraries it loads beside it, read with `otool -L`; the models in `Contents/Resources`) and its disk image, signed ad hoc until there is a Developer ID; `packaging/macos/` has the Info.plist, the two icons (`AppIcon.icon` for macOS 26 and later, compiled by `actool` into `Assets.car`; `AppIcon.icns` before), `smoke.sh` (the disk image checked as a person meets it: the signature, both architectures, the libraries, the oldest macOS; llama-server answers a chat with Bonsai 1.7B and the window stays open, natively and under Rosetta) and `release-upload.sh`; `.github/workflows/macos.yml` runs it on the project's Mac, a self-hosted runner of that private repository, when asked (the Actions tab, or a push to `macos-build`), builds the application from `app/<ref>` while it is private, sends the results back as the branch `macos-results` (`report.sh`) and attaches the disk image to a draft release; `build/config.env` pins the two macOS builds and the oldest macOS (13.3) |

## 14. New dependencies, by phase

| Phase | Crate | Where | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `toml`, `toml_edit`, `serde_ignored`, `regex`, `semver` | `quickai-core` | parsing and merging, line lookup, unknown keys, `matches`, `semver_ge` |
| 3 | `sha2`, `ruzstd`, `libc` (Unix), `windows-sys` (Windows) | `quickai` | digests, `.tar.zst`, process groups and `PDEATHSIG`, job objects. The tests added none: the kit uses `tiny_http`, `ureq`, `tar`, `flate2`, `zip` and the system `zstd` when it is installed |
| 5 | `schemars`, `include_dir` (if not earlier) | build and docs, `quickai` | JSON Schema, embedded manifests |

Each addition is checked for licence compatibility (dependencies must be permissively licensed) and for Windows, macOS and old-glibc
Linux builds before it is merged.

## 15. Spike track (S)

Runs by hand in an isolated `HOME`, results recorded in `engine-facts.md`. Not on the critical path of Phases
0-2. **Status (2026-10-06):** the Phase 3 items are settled except A8; A12 of the Phase 4 items is settled as well
(the table in `engine-facts.md` has the findings and how each was measured). Open: A8 and A11 need Windows, A10
needs two distant llama.cpp builds, A13 needs a model and projector that belong together, A15 and A16 are Phase 6.

| Needed before | Items |
|---|---|
| Phase 3 | A1 (archive layout under an arbitrary prefix), A2 (Vulkan in the Linux archive), A3 (SIGTERM and SIGKILL behaviour), A4 (`~/.ollama` key writes), A5 (Bonsai Q1_0 through `/api/create`), A6 (`OLLAMA_NO_CLOUD`), A7 (`OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH`), A8 (Windows console window), A9 (`ruzstd` throughput), A14 (Ollama API details), A17 (`ollama --version` output) |
| Phase 4 | A10 (flag drift between distant llama.cpp builds), A11 (`--lora-scaled` with a Windows path), A12 (projector mismatch error text), A13 (`/props` modality keys) |
| Phase 6 | A15 (router mode load and unload), A16 (sidecar flags) |

## 16. Resuming

A fresh session should read, in order: this file's status table and logs, `architecture.md` §15 and the section of
the current phase, `engine-facts.md` for the assumption numbers involved, and `git status` to see what is in the
working tree. For Phase 3 also [phase3-test-plan.md](phase3-test-plan.md): running its red tests (§4 above) shows
where the work stands, and each work package removes the ignores of its own rows. Then run the gate commands of §4
to confirm the starting point is green. The application is four crates since WP4.18: the tests of everything that does anything to the computer are `cargo test -p quickai-runtime` (and `-p quickai-core` for the pure part), the window's own are `-p quickai --bin quickai`, and the headless program's are `-p quickai-run`; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` runs them all.
